    95→def test_batch_prompt_requires_score_and_finding_consistency():
    96→    prompt = batch_core_mod.build_batch_prompt(
    97→        repo_root=Path("/repo"),
    98→        packet_path=Path("/repo/.desloppify/review_packets/p.json"),
    99→        batch_index=0,
   100→        batch={
   101→            "name": "Architecture & Coupling",
   102→            "dimensions": ["high_level_elegance"],
   103→            "why": "test",
   104→            "files_to_read": ["core.py", "scan.py"],
   105→        },
   106→    )
   107→    assert "Score/finding consistency is required" in prompt
   108→
   109→
   110→def test_dimension_merge_scorer_penalizes_higher_pressure():
   111→    scorer = DimensionMergeScorer()
   112→    low = scorer.score_dimension(
   113→        ScoreInputs(
   114→            weighted_mean=92.0,
   115→            floor=90.0,
   116→            finding_pressure=1.0,
   117→            finding_count=1,
   118→        )
   119→    )
   120→    high = scorer.score_dimension(
   121→        ScoreInputs(
   122→            weighted_mean=92.0,
   123→            floor=90.0,
   124→            finding_pressure=4.08,
   125→            finding_count=1,
   126→        )
   127→    )
   128→    assert low.final_score > high.final_score
   129→
   130→
   131→def test_dimension_merge_scorer_penalizes_additional_findings():
   132→    scorer = DimensionMergeScorer()
   133→    one_finding = scorer.score_dimension(
   134→        ScoreInputs(
   135→            weighted_mean=92.0,
   136→            floor=90.0,
   137→            finding_pressure=2.0,
   138→            finding_count=1,
   139→        )
   140→    )
   141→    three_findings = scorer.score_dimension(
   142→        ScoreInputs(
   143→            weighted_mean=92.0,
   144→            floor=90.0,
   145→            finding_pressure=2.0,
   146→            finding_count=3,
   147→        )
   148→    )
   149→    assert one_finding.final_score > three_findings.final_score
   150→
   151→
   152→def test_merge_batch_results_merges_same_identifier_findings():
   153→    merged = _merge(
   154→        [
   155→            {
   156→                "assessments": {"logic_clarity": 70.0},
   157→                "dimension_notes": {
   158→                    "logic_clarity": {
   159→                        "evidence": ["predicate mismatch in task filtering"],
   160→                        "impact_scope": "module",
   161→                        "fix_scope": "single_edit",
   162→                        "confidence": "medium",
   163→                        "issues_preventing_higher_score": "",
   164→                    }
   165→                },
   166→                "findings": [
   167→                    {
   168→                        "dimension": "logic_clarity",
   169→                        "identifier": "processing_filter_predicate_mismatch",
   170→                        "summary": "Mismatch in processing predicates",
   171→                        "related_files": ["src/a.ts", "src/b.ts"],
   172→                        "evidence": ["branch A uses OR"],
   173→                        "suggestion": "align predicates",
   174→                        "confidence": "high",
   175→                        "impact_scope": "module",
   176→                        "fix_scope": "single_edit",
   177→                    }
   178→                ],
   179→                "quality": {},
   180→            },
   181→            {
   182→                "assessments": {"logic_clarity": 65.0},
   183→                "dimension_notes": {
   184→                    "logic_clarity": {
   185→                        "evidence": ["task filtering diverges"],
   186→                        "impact_scope": "module",
   187→                        "fix_scope": "single_edit",
   188→                        "confidence": "medium",
   189→                        "issues_preventing_higher_score": "",
   190→                    }
   191→                },
   192→                "findings": [
   193→                    {
   194→                        "dimension": "logic_clarity",
   195→                        "identifier": "processing_filter_predicate_mismatch",
   196→                        "summary": "Processing predicate mismatch across hooks",
   197→                        "related_files": ["src/b.ts", "src/c.ts"],
   198→                        "evidence": ["branch B uses AND"],
   199→                        "suggestion": "create shared predicate helper",
   200→                        "confidence": "high",
   201→                        "impact_scope": "module",
   202→                        "fix_scope": "single_edit",
   203→                    }
   204→                ],
   205→                "quality": {},
   206→            },
   207→        ]
   208→    )
   209→    findings = merged["findings"]
   210→    assert len(findings) == 1
   211→    finding = findings[0]
   212→    assert finding["identifier"] == "processing_filter_predicate_mismatch"
   213→    assert finding["summary"] == "Processing predicate mismatch across hooks"
   214→    assert set(finding["related_files"]) == {"src/a.ts", "src/b.ts", "src/c.ts"}
   215→    assert set(finding["evidence"]) == {"branch A uses OR", "branch B uses AND"}
   216→

   255→    _assert_all_callables(

   271→# Behavioral tests for key functions (beyond assert callable)
   272→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

   274→
   275→def test_noise_budget_defaults():
   276→    """resolve_finding_noise_budget returns default for None config."""
   277→    assert noise.resolve_finding_noise_budget(None) == 10
   278→    assert noise.resolve_finding_noise_budget({}) == 10
   279→
   280→
   281→def test_noise_budget_from_config():
   282→    """resolve_finding_noise_budget reads the config value."""
   283→    assert noise.resolve_finding_noise_budget({"finding_noise_budget": 5}) == 5
   284→    assert noise.resolve_finding_noise_budget({"finding_noise_budget": 0}) == 0
   285→
   286→
   287→def test_noise_settings_invalid_config():
   288→    """resolve_finding_noise_settings returns warning for invalid values."""
   289→    per, glob, warning = noise.resolve_finding_noise_settings(
   290→        {"finding_noise_budget": "bad"}
   291→    )
   292→    assert per == 10  # default
   293→    assert warning is not None
   294→    assert "Invalid" in warning
   295→
   296→
   297→def test_serialize_item_minimal():
   298→    """serialize_item extracts expected fields from a minimal item dict."""
   299→    item = {
   300→        "id": "smells::foo.py::1",
   301→        "kind": "finding",
   302→        "tier": 2,
   303→        "confidence": "high",
   304→        "detector": "smells",
   305→        "file": "foo.py",
   306→        "summary": "Unused import",
   307→        "status": "open",
   308→    }
   309→    result = next_output.serialize_item(item)
   310→    assert result["id"] == "smells::foo.py::1"
   311→    assert result["kind"] == "finding"
   312→    assert result["tier"] == 2
   313→    assert result["detector"] == "smells"
   314→    assert result["file"] == "foo.py"
   315→    assert "explain" not in result
   316→
   317→
   318→def test_build_query_payload_structure():
   319→    """build_query_payload returns well-formed dict with queue metadata."""
   320→    items = [{"id": "f1", "kind": "finding", "tier": 1}]
   321→    queue = {"tier_counts": {1: 1}, "total": 1}
   322→    payload = next_output.build_query_payload(
   323→        queue, items, command="next", narrative=None
   324→    )
   325→    assert payload["command"] == "next"
   326→    assert len(payload["items"]) == 1
   327→    assert payload["queue"]["total"] == 1
   328→    assert payload["narrative"] is None
   329→
   330→
   331→def test_private_imports_is_dunder():
   332→    """_is_dunder correctly identifies dunder names."""
   333→    assert private_imports._is_dunder("__all__") is True
   334→    assert private_imports._is_dunder("__init__") is True
   335→    assert private_imports._is_dunder("_private") is False
   336→    assert private_imports._is_dunder("public") is False
   337→
   338→
   339→def test_command_registry_has_core_commands():
   340→    """get_command_handlers includes scan, status, next, plan."""
   341→    handlers = cmd_registry.get_command_handlers()
   342→    for cmd in ("scan", "status", "next", "plan"):
   343→        assert cmd in handlers, f"Missing command handler: {cmd}"
   344→        assert callable(handlers[cmd])
   345→

Here is the detailed refactoring plan for `runner_helpers.py`.

---

## Refactoring Plan for `runner_helpers.py`

### Overview

The refactoring targets three problem areas: (1) the monolithic `run_codex_batch` function with its 6 nested closures and mutable-list hacks, (2) the duplicated execution paths (Popen vs subprocess.run), and (3) the nested `_extract_payload_from_log` inside `collect_batch_results`. The strategy is to introduce two small, focused internal classes and promote closures to module-level functions.

---

### Step 1: Introduce `_RunnerState` dataclass to replace mutable-list hacks

**What it does:** Replaces `runner_note: list[str] = []` (used as `runner_note[:]`) and `last_stream_activity = [started_monotonic]` (used as `last_stream_activity[0]`) with a proper mutable state container. All per-attempt mutable state that closures currently capture via list-cell hacks moves here.

**New code:**

```python
@dataclass
class _RunnerState:
    """Mutable per-attempt state shared between the main loop and helper threads."""
    stdout_chunks: list[str]
    stderr_chunks: list[str]
    runner_note: str
    last_stream_activity: float
    lock: threading.Lock
    stop_event: threading.Event
```

**Fields come from these current locals:**
- `stdout_chunks: list[str]` -- was already a list, no change in semantics
- `stderr_chunks: list[str]` -- same
- `runner_note: str` -- was `runner_note: list[str] = []`, accessed as `runner_note[0]` / `runner_note[:] = [...]`. Now just a string attribute: `state.runner_note = "..."`
- `last_stream_activity: float` -- was `last_stream_activity = [started_monotonic]`, accessed as `last_stream_activity[0]`. Now `state.last_stream_activity`
- `lock: threading.Lock` -- was `state_lock`
- `stop_event: threading.Event` -- was `stop_live_writer`

**Why a dataclass:** It is simple, no methods needed, and it is internal (underscore-prefixed). Using `frozen=False` since the whole point is mutability.

---

### Step 2: Extract closure `_output_file_status_text` to module-level function

**New name:** `_output_file_status_text(output_file: Path) -> str`

**Current closure captures:** `output_file` (from `run_codex_batch` parameters).

**After extraction:** Takes `output_file` as an explicit parameter. The function is pure -- it only reads from `output_file` via stat/exists.

**Callers:** `_write_live_snapshot` (which itself will become a module-level function).

---

### Step 3: Extract closure `_output_file_has_json_payload` to module-level function

**New name:** `_output_file_has_json_payload(output_file: Path) -> bool`

**Current closure captures:** `output_file`.

**After extraction:** Takes `output_file` as an explicit parameter. Pure function.

**Callers:** The stall-recovery check (line 351), the timeout recovery check (line 373), and the stall-recovered check (line 388).

---

### Step 4: Extract closure `_terminate_process` to module-level function

**New name:** `_terminate_process(process: subprocess.Popen[str]) -> None`

**Current closure captures:** Nothing (it only uses its parameter). This is already essentially a static helper that happens to be defined inside the function.

**After extraction:** No signature change needed -- it already takes `process` as a parameter with no closure captures. Move as-is to module scope.

---

### Step 5: Extract closure `_drain_stream` to module-level function

**New name:** `_drain_stream(stream, sink: list[str], state: _RunnerState) -> None`

**Current closure captures:** `state_lock` and `last_stream_activity` (the mutable list cell).

**After extraction:** Takes `state: _RunnerState` instead of capturing the lock and activity tracker via closure. The body changes from:
```python
with state_lock:
    sink.append(chunk)
    last_stream_activity[0] = time.monotonic()
```
to:
```python
with state.lock:
    sink.append(chunk)
    state.last_stream_activity = time.monotonic()
```

---

### Step 6: Extract closure `_write_live_snapshot` to module-level function

**New name:** `_write_live_snapshot(*, state: _RunnerState, header: str, started_at_iso: str, started_monotonic: float, output_file: Path, log_file: Path, log_sections: list[str], safe_write_text_fn) -> None`

**Current closure captures:** `output_file`, `log_file`, `deps`, `log_sections`, `header`, `started_at_iso`, `started_monotonic`, `stdout_chunks`, `stderr_chunks`, `runner_note`, `state_lock`.

**After extraction:** The `_RunnerState` replaces `stdout_chunks`, `stderr_chunks`, `runner_note`, and `state_lock`. The remaining captured variables (`header`, `started_at_iso`, `started_monotonic`, `output_file`, `log_file`, `log_sections`, `safe_write_text_fn`) become explicit keyword parameters.

**Why keyword-only:** There are enough parameters that positional calling would be error-prone. A `_LiveSnapshotContext` dataclass grouping the immutable per-attempt values is an alternative if the parameter count feels excessive (see Alternative below).

**Alternative -- bundle immutable per-attempt context:**

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class _AttemptContext:
    header: str
    started_at_iso: str
    started_monotonic: float
    output_file: Path
    log_file: Path
    log_sections: list[str]  # shared across attempts, mutable
    safe_write_text_fn: object
```

This reduces `_write_live_snapshot` to `_write_live_snapshot(state: _RunnerState, ctx: _AttemptContext)`. I recommend this approach since `_start_live_writer` also needs the same bundle.

---

### Step 7: Extract closure `_start_live_writer` to module-level function

**New name:** `_start_live_writer(*, state: _RunnerState, ctx: _AttemptContext, live_log_interval_seconds: float) -> threading.Thread`

**Current closure captures:** `stop_live_writer`, `live_log_interval_seconds`, plus everything `_write_live_snapshot` captures.

**After extraction:** Takes the state, context, and interval. The inner `_loop` lambda becomes:

```python
def _loop() -> None:
    while not state.stop_event.wait(live_log_interval_seconds):
        _write_live_snapshot(state=state, ctx=ctx)
```

---

### Step 8: Introduce `_ExecutionResult` dataclass to unify the two execution paths

**New code:**

```python
@dataclass
class _ExecutionResult:
    """Normalized outcome from either Popen or subprocess.run path."""
    code: int
    stdout_text: str
    stderr_text: str
    timed_out: bool = False
    recovered_from_stall: bool = False
    early_return: int | None = None  # If set, skip post-processing and return this code
```

**Purpose:** Both the Popen path (lines 252-401) and the subprocess.run path (lines 402-441) currently produce the same outputs: `stdout_text`, `stderr_text`, `code`, plus conditional early returns for timeout/stall/errors. Unifying them into a return value lets the shared post-processing logic (lines 443-466: success check, transient failure detection, retry backoff) live in one place.

---

### Step 9: Extract the Popen execution path to `_run_via_popen`

**New name:** `_run_via_popen(*, cmd: list[str], deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps, state: _RunnerState, ctx: _AttemptContext, live_log_interval_seconds: float, stall_after_output_seconds: int) -> _ExecutionResult`

**What moves:** Lines 252-401 (the `if use_popen_runner:` branch). This includes:
- Process creation with `deps.subprocess_popen`
- Starting drain threads via `_drain_stream`
- The stall detection poll loop
- Process cleanup
- Timeout/stall log section appending

**Stall detection flattening (sub-step):** The current stall detection loop (lines 314-354) has 4-5 levels of nesting:
```
while process.poll() is None:
    if elapsed >= ...:          # level 2
    if stall_after_output_seconds > 0:  # level 2
        if current_signature is None:     # level 3
        elif ...:                         # level 3
        elif ...:                         # level 3
            if output_age >= ... and ...:   # level 4
```

Flatten by extracting a helper `_check_stall(*, output_file, output_signature, output_stable_since, now_monotonic, last_stream_activity, stall_after_output_seconds) -> tuple[tuple[int,int]|None, float|None, bool]` that returns the updated signature, stable_since, and a boolean `should_terminate`. This converts the nested conditionals into a single function call in the loop:

```python
output_signature, output_stable_since, stall_detected = _check_stall(
    output_file=ctx.output_file,
    output_signature=output_signature,
    output_stable_since=output_stable_since,
    now_monotonic=now_monotonic,
    last_stream_activity=state.last_stream_activity,
    stall_after_output_seconds=stall_after_output_seconds,
)
if stall_detected:
    state.runner_note = f"stall recovery triggered after {stall_after_output_seconds}s ..."
    recovered_from_stall = _output_file_has_json_payload(ctx.output_file)
    _terminate_process(process)
    break
```

**Error handling:** `OSError` and general `Exception` from `subprocess_popen` currently append to `log_sections` and do early returns (127, 1). In the extracted function, these become `_ExecutionResult(code=127, ..., early_return=127)`.

---

### Step 10: Extract the subprocess.run execution path to `_run_via_subprocess`

**New name:** `_run_via_subprocess(*, cmd: list[str], deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps, state: _RunnerState, ctx: _AttemptContext, live_log_interval_seconds: float) -> _ExecutionResult`

**What moves:** Lines 402-441 (the `else:` branch).

**Error handling:** `deps.timeout_error`, `OSError`, `RuntimeError|ValueError` exceptions currently append to `log_sections` and do early returns. In the extracted function, these return `_ExecutionResult(early_return=124)`, `_ExecutionResult(early_return=127)`, `_ExecutionResult(early_return=1)` respectively.

---

### Step 11: Simplify `run_codex_batch` to orchestration-only

After extractions, the function body becomes approximately:

```python
def run_codex_batch(*, prompt, repo_root, output_file, log_file, deps) -> int:
    cmd = codex_batch_command(...)
    # Validate/normalize config (lines 137-157 stay, they're simple)
    max_attempts = ...
    log_sections: list[str] = []

    for attempt in range(1, max_attempts + 1):
        # Build per-attempt context
        ctx = _AttemptContext(
            header=f"ATTEMPT {attempt}/{max_attempts}\n$ {' '.join(cmd)}",
            started_at_iso=datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
            started_monotonic=time.monotonic(),
            output_file=output_file,
            log_file=log_file,
            log_sections=log_sections,
            safe_write_text_fn=deps.safe_write_text_fn,
        )
        state = _RunnerState(
            stdout_chunks=[], stderr_chunks=[],
            runner_note="",
            last_stream_activity=ctx.started_monotonic,
            lock=threading.Lock(),
            stop_event=threading.Event(),
        )

        _write_live_snapshot(state=state, ctx=ctx)

        use_popen = bool(deps.use_popen_runner) and callable(getattr(deps, "subprocess_popen", None))
        if use_popen:
            result = _run_via_popen(cmd=cmd, deps=deps, state=state, ctx=ctx, ...)
        else:
            result = _run_via_subprocess(cmd=cmd, deps=deps, state=state, ctx=ctx, ...)

        if result.early_return is not None:
            return result.early_return

        # Shared post-processing (lines 443-466)
        if result.code == 0:
            deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
            return 0
        # Transient failure retry logic...
        ...

    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
    return 1
```

This reduces `run_codex_batch` from ~340 lines to approximately 60-70 lines of clear orchestration logic.

---

### Step 12: Extract `_extract_payload_from_log` out of `collect_batch_results`

**New name:** `_extract_payload_from_log(*, batch_index: int, raw_path: Path, extract_payload_fn) -> dict[str, object] | None`

**Current closure captures:** `extract_payload_fn` (from `collect_batch_results` parameters).

**After extraction:** `extract_payload_fn` becomes an explicit keyword parameter. `idx` becomes `batch_index` for clarity. The function body is unchanged (lines 843-878).

**Call site change:** In `collect_batch_results`, line 893 changes from:
```python
payload = _extract_payload_from_log(idx, raw_path)
```
to:
```python
payload = _extract_payload_from_log(
    batch_index=idx,
    raw_path=raw_path,
    extract_payload_fn=extract_payload_fn,
)
```

---

### Step 13: Flatten the stall detection logic via `_check_stall`

**New name:** `_check_stall(*, output_file: Path, prev_signature: tuple[int, int] | None, prev_stable_since: float | None, now_monotonic: float, last_stream_activity: float, stall_after_output_seconds: int) -> tuple[tuple[int, int] | None, float | None, bool]`

**Returns:** `(new_signature, new_stable_since, should_terminate)`

**What it encapsulates:** Lines 323-353 -- the entire stall detection conditional tree. The 4-level nesting becomes a flat sequence of early returns:

```python
def _check_stall(...) -> tuple[...]:
    try:
        stat = output_file.stat()
        current_signature = (int(stat.st_size), int(stat.st_mtime))
    except OSError:
        return None, None, False

    if current_signature != prev_signature:
        return current_signature, now_monotonic, False

    if prev_stable_since is None:
        return current_signature, None, False

    output_age = now_monotonic - prev_stable_since
    stream_idle = now_monotonic - last_stream_activity
    should_terminate = (
        output_age >= stall_after_output_seconds
        and stream_idle >= stall_after_output_seconds
    )
    return current_signature, prev_stable_since, should_terminate
```

---

### Summary of new module-level symbols (all private)

| Symbol | Kind | Lines it replaces |
|---|---|---|
| `_RunnerState` | `dataclass` | `runner_note`, `last_stream_activity`, `state_lock`, `stop_live_writer`, `stdout_chunks`, `stderr_chunks` locals |
| `_AttemptContext` | `dataclass(frozen=True)` | Immutable per-attempt values captured by closures |
| `_ExecutionResult` | `dataclass` | Unifies return path from both execution strategies |
| `_output_file_status_text(output_file)` | function | Lines 160-173 closure |
| `_output_file_has_json_payload(output_file)` | function | Lines 175-182 closure |
| `_terminate_process(process)` | function | Lines 184-197 closure |
| `_drain_stream(stream, sink, state)` | function | Lines 282-299 closure |
| `_write_live_snapshot(state, ctx)` | function | Lines 210-234 closure |
| `_start_live_writer(state, ctx, interval)` | function | Lines 236-243 closure |
| `_run_via_popen(cmd, deps, state, ctx, ...)` | function | Lines 252-401 Popen branch |
| `_run_via_subprocess(cmd, deps, state, ctx, ...)` | function | Lines 402-441 subprocess.run branch |
| `_check_stall(...)` | function | Lines 323-353 nested conditionals |
| `_extract_payload_from_log(batch_index, raw_path, extract_payload_fn)` | function | Lines 843-878 nested function |

---

### Execution order and dependencies

1. **Steps 1, 8 first** -- Define `_RunnerState`, `_AttemptContext`, `_ExecutionResult` dataclasses. These have no dependencies.
2. **Steps 2, 3, 4 next** -- Extract `_output_file_status_text`, `_output_file_has_json_payload`, `_terminate_process`. These are leaf functions with no dependencies on the new dataclasses.
3. **Step 5** -- Extract `_drain_stream`. Depends on `_RunnerState`.
4. **Steps 6, 7** -- Extract `_write_live_snapshot`, `_start_live_writer`. Depend on `_RunnerState`, `_AttemptContext`, and `_output_file_status_text`.
5. **Step 13** -- Extract `_check_stall`. Standalone.
6. **Steps 9, 10** -- Extract `_run_via_popen`, `_run_via_subprocess`. Depend on all the above.
7. **Step 11** -- Rewrite `run_codex_batch` to use the extracted pieces. This is the integration step.
8. **Step 12** -- Extract `_extract_payload_from_log`. Independent of steps 1-11.

Each step should be followed by a test run (`python -m pytest desloppify/tests/review/test_review_commands.py -q -x`) to verify no regressions.

---

### What stays unchanged

- `__all__` exports -- no additions, no removals.
- `CodexBatchRunnerDeps` -- frozen dataclass, all fields identical.
- `BatchResult` -- frozen dataclass, unchanged.
- `run_codex_batch` signature and return codes (0, 1, 124, 127).
- `collect_batch_results` signature and return type.
- All other public functions in the file.
- The dep-injection pattern -- tests inject mocked `CodexBatchRunnerDeps` exactly as before. The `use_popen_runner=False` path dispatches to `_run_via_subprocess`; the `use_popen_runner=True` path dispatches to `_run_via_popen`.

| `test_run_codex_batch_retries_stream_disconnect` | `use_popen_runner=False` path, transient failure retry | Tests the retry loop in `run_codex_batch`. No logic changes in retry handling. Pass unchanged. |
| `test_run_codex_batch_writes_live_status_before_completion` | `use_popen_runner=False` path, live snapshot written before run | Tests `_write_live_snapshot`. The snapshot logic is extracted but not changed. Pass unchanged. |
| `test_run_codex_batch_stall_recovery_from_output_file` | `use_popen_runner=True` path, stall detection + recovery | Tests `_run_via_popen` with `_check_stall`. Logic preserved exactly. Pass unchanged. |
| `test_collect_batch_results_recovers_execution_failure_with_valid_output` | Output file present despite execution failure | Tests `collect_batch_results`. Only `_extract_payload_from_log` extraction changes, and that path is not hit (output file exists). Pass unchanged. |
| `test_collect_batch_results_skips_full_log_fallback_when_stdout_empty` | Log fallback with empty STDOUT section | Tests `_extract_payload_from_log` (now at module level). Logic preserved exactly. Pass unchanged. |

| `test_print_failures_*` (5 tests) | Failure reporting and hints | No changes to failure reporting functions. Pass unchanged. |

### Risks and mitigations

1. **Thread safety of `_RunnerState`:** The current code already uses `state_lock` for all mutations to `stdout_chunks`, `stderr_chunks`, `runner_note`, and `last_stream_activity`. The refactored code uses `state.lock` identically. No change in thread-safety properties.

2. **Parameter count on extracted functions:** `_write_live_snapshot` would have 8+ parameters without `_AttemptContext`. With the context dataclass, it drops to 2. This is why `_AttemptContext` is worth the overhead.

3. **`_ExecutionResult.early_return` pattern:** This is a pragmatic way to unify the two paths without changing the control flow semantics. The alternative (raising a custom exception to signal early return) would be less readable. The `early_return` field is checked exactly once in the orchestrator.

4. **Closures referencing `log_sections`:** Both `_write_live_snapshot` and the two execution path functions mutate `log_sections` (appending section strings). Since `log_sections` is passed through `_AttemptContext` (which holds a reference to the mutable list), the append semantics are preserved without closure capture.
